# TEXT OF AMENDMENTS

**Measures:** H.R. 1, S.Con.Res. 33

```
                           TEXT OF AMENDMENTS

  SA 4794. Mr. HAWLEY proposed an amendment to the concurrent 
resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for 
the United States Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the 
appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO EXTENDING 
                   THE PROHIBITION ON MEDICAID PAYMENTS TO 
                   ABORTION PROVIDERS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     extending the prohibition on Federal payments to abortion 
     providers under the Medicaid program, by the amounts provided 
     in such legislation for those purposes, provided that such 
     legislation would not increase the deficit over the period of 
     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 4795. Mrs. SHAHEEN (for herself, Ms. Baldwin, Ms. Alsobrooks, and 
Mr. Warnock) submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by her to 
the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO RESTORING 
                   HEALTH CARE ACCESS AND AFFORDABILITY FOR 
                   BENEFICIARIES OF ENHANCED ADVANCED PREMIUM TAX 
                   CREDITS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     securing health care access and affordability for Americans, 
     which may include restoring enhanced advanced premium tax 
     credits, whose loss has created catastrophic insurance 
     premium hikes for 24,000,000 Americans and the loss of 
     insurance coverage for an additional 4,000,000 Americans, or 
     ensuring that any changes would not result in lower coverage 
     rates, reduced benefits, or decreased affordability for 
     beneficiaries receiving coverage through private insurance 
     markets, by the amounts provided in such legislation for 
     those purposes, provided that such legislation would not 
     increase the deficit over the period of the total of fiscal 
     years 2026 through 2035.
                                 ______

by her to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO ENSURING 
                   THAT TARIFF REFUNDS ARE PROCESSED AS EASILY AS 
                   POSSIBLE FOR SMALL BUSINESSES.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     ensuring that refunds of unlawful tariffs are processed as 
     quickly and easily as possible for small businesses, which 
     may include automatic refunds using information that U.S. 
     Customs and Border Protection already possesses, by the 
     amounts provided in such legislation for those purposes, 
     provided that such legislation would not increase the deficit 
     over the period of the total of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.
                                 ______

                   TAX CREDITS THAT HELP FAMILIES LOWER THEIR 
                   ENERGY BILLS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees,

     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or

     2035.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate

  SA 4799. Mr. SCHUMER (for himself, Ms. Alsobrooks, Mr. Kim, and Mr. 
Lujan) proposed an amendment to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 
33, setting forth the congressional budget for the United States 
Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate 
budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; as follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

[[Page S1950]]

  


     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT DOES NOT LOWER OUT-OF-POCKET HEALTH CARE 
                   COSTS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that does not lower out-of-pocket health 
     care costs.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     restoring the enhanced premium assistance tax credits for 1 
     year by the amounts provided in such legislation for those 
     purposes, provided that such legislation would not increase 
     the deficit over the period of the total of fiscal years 2026 
     through 2035.

  SA 4821. Ms. HIRONO (for herself and Mr. Van Hollen) submitted an 
amendment intended to be proposed by her to

[[Page S1953]]

the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT WOULD NOT INCREASE FEDERAL FUNDING FOR 
                   LOW-INCOME STUDENTS AND STUDENTS WITH 
                   DISABILITIES.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that would not increase Federal funding for 
     low-income students and students with disabilities.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4825. Mr. BLUMENTHAL (for himself, Mr. Gallego, Ms. Hirono, and 
Mr. King) submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by him to the 
concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the congressional 
budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 2026 and 
setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 
through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as follows:

       Strike titles II and III and insert the following:

                        TITLE II--RECONCILIATION

     SEC. 2001. RECONCILIATION IN THE HOUSE OF REPRESENTATIVES.

       In the House of Representatives, not later than May 15, 
     2026, the Committee on Armed Services shall report to the 
     House of Representatives changes in laws within its 
     jurisdiction that increase the deficit by not more than 
     $13,000,000,000 for the period of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.

     SEC. 2002. RECONCILIATION IN THE SENATE.

       In the Senate, not later than May 15, 2026, the Committee 
     on Armed Services shall report to the Senate changes in laws 
     within its jurisdiction that increase the deficit by not more 
     than $13,000,000,000 for the period of fiscal years 2026 
     through 2035.

                        TITLE III--RESERVE FUNDS

     SEC. 3001. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO PROVIDING 
                   COMBAT-INJURED MILITARY RETIREES WITH THEIR 
                   FULL MILITARY BENEFITS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     enhancing benefits for combat-injured veterans, which may 
     include legislation to ensure veterans entitled to disability 
     compensation from the Department of Veterans Affairs and 
     retirement payments from the Department of Defense receive 
     such compensation and payments concurrently, or eliminate the 
     offset of Department of Veterans Affairs disability 
     compensation and Department of Defense retirement payments 
     for veterans who were medically retired from service in the 
     Armed Forces and have a combat-related disability, by the 
     amounts provided in such legislation for those purposes, 
     provided that such legislation would not increase the deficit 
     over the period of the total of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.
                                 ______

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3003. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   SUPPORTING DUE PROCESS AND UNBIASED IMMIGRATION 
                   JUDGES.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     supporting immigration judges through training, funding, and 
     hiring practices that encourage immigration judges to comply 
     with due process, issue unbiased judgements aligned with 
     current immigration laws, and stand against political 
     pressures that would unduly influence their ability to 
     provide judicial relief in immigration cases, including 
     requests for asylum, by the amounts provided in such 
     legislation for those purposes, provided that such 
     legislation would not increase the deficit over the period of 
     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT MANDATES 
                   THE SALE OF FEDERAL PUBLIC LAND WITHOUT PUBLIC 
                   NOTICE OR ENVIRONMENTAL REVIEW.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that 
     mandates the sale of Federal public land without public 
     notice or environmental review.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4855. Mr. PADILLA submitted an amendment intended to be proposed 
by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT WOULD PROVIDE FUNDING TO FEDERAL AGENCIES 
                   THAT HAVE UNOBLIGATED FUNDS FROM PREVIOUS 
                   RECONCILIATION BILLS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on,

[[Page S1958]]

     or amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that would provide funding to a Federal 
     agency that has unobligated funds made available to the 
     Federal agency under 1 or more previous reconciliation bills.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or

     such as last names, badge identification numbers, and 
     employing agency, or prohibiting the use of masks, 
     plainclothes, and paramilitary uniforms, by the amounts 
     provided in such legislation for those purposes, provided 
     that such legislation would not increase the deficit over the 
     period of the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   PROTECTING CIVIL LIBERTIES AND HUMAN DIGNITY BY 
                   ENSURING BASIC DETENTION STANDARDS AND 
                   PREVENTING THE ARREST OR DETENTION OF UNITED 
                   STATES CITIZENS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     protecting civil liberties and human dignity, which may 
     include ensuring basic detention standards across detention 
     facilities, preventing the arrest or detention of United 
     States citizens, requiring immediate access to an 
     individual's attorney, or allowing States to sue the 
     Department of Homeland Security for violations of such 
     requirements, by the amounts provided in such legislation for 
     those purposes, provided that such legislation would not 
     increase the deficit over the period of the total of fiscal 
     years 2026 through 2035.

  SA 4863. Mr. WYDEN submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT WOULD NOT SUPPORT FIRST-TIME HOMEBUYERS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that would not help first-time homebuyers 
     with the cost of purchasing a home.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

                   EMPLOYMENT AUTHORIZATION DOCUMENT WHEN A

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate

     Employment Authorization Document when a qualified immigrant 
     applies for such extension in a timely manner.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

                   NOT PROHIBIT IMMIGRATION ENFORCEMENT ACTIONS BY 
                   DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND SECURITY OFFICERS AND 
                   AGENTS WITHIN 1,000 FEET OF ANY SENSITIVE 
                   LOCATION.

         (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the 
     Senate to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, 
     amendment, amendment between the Houses, or conference report 
     that does not prohibit immigration enforcement actions by 
     Department of Homeland Security officers and agents within 
     1,000 feet of any sensitive location, including schools, 
     medical facilities, places of worship, child care facilities, 
     and courthouses.
         (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4897. Mr. OSSOFF proposed an amendment to the concurrent 
resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for 
the United States Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the 
appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT FAILS TO PREVENT THE IMPROPER DENIAL OR 
                   DELAY OF MEDICALLY NECESSARY HEALTH CARE BY 
                   INSURANCE COMPANIES.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that fails to hold health insurance 
     companies accountable for improperly denying or delaying 
     medically necessary health care, including cancer treatment, 
     organ transplantation, and mental health care for children.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

  SA 4922. Ms. HIRONO submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
her to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT SPENDS 
                   MORE TAXPAYER DOLLARS ON IMMIGRATION 
                   ENFORCEMENT THAN ON THE NATIONAL AERONAUTICS 
                   AND SPACE ADMINISTRATION.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     provide more funding for civil immigration enforcement than 
     funding provided to the National Aeronautics and Space 
     Administration in the same fiscal year.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

                   MAINTAINING A DATABASE OF INDIVIDUALS 
                   PARTICIPATING IN ACTIVITIES PROTECTED BY THE 
                   FIRST AMENDMENT TO THE CONSTITUTION OF THE 
                   UNITED STATES.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that 
     provides funding that can be used to track, create, or 
     maintain a database of individuals participating in 
     activities protected by the First Amendment to the 
     Constitution of the United States.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4939. Mr. VAN HOLLEN submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST THE CANCELLATION OF CLEAN 
                   ENERGY PROJECTS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that uses 
     funds for the cancellation of clean energy projects, 
     including refunds on lease areas and remand or cancellation 
     of Federal permits.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4940. Mr. VAN HOLLEN submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for

as follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST DIRECT SPENDING FOR THE 
                   OFFICE OF MANAGEMENT AND BUDGET.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that 
     provides an increase in direct spending (as defined in 
     section 250(c) of the Balanced Budget and Emergency Deficit 
     Control Act of 1985 (2 U.S.C. 900(c)) for the Office of 
     Management and Budget.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate

     fund Federal agencies engaged in expungement of convictions 
     of people convicted of seditious conspiracy or assaulting law

       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

as follows:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST FUNDING FEDERAL AGENCIES 
                   THAT DO NOT COOPERATE WITH STATE AND LOCAL LAW 
                   ENFORCEMENT AGENCIES IN INVESTIGATIONS INTO 
                   DEATHS AND SERIOUS BODILY HARM.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     fund Federal agencies without clear policies to share 
     evidence and cooperate in investigations by State and local 
     law enforcement agencies into the death of or serious bodily 
     harm caused to residents of those jurisdictions.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 4959. Ms. BLUNT ROCHESTER submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by her to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4109. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT WOULD INCREASE THE DEFICIT FOR IMMIGRATION 
                   ENFORCEMENT WHILE HOUSING PRICES CONTINUE TO 
                   OUTPACE INCOME GROWTH.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that would increase the deficit for 
     immigration enforcement while housing prices continue to 
     outpace income growth.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______

  SA 4980. Mr. PADILLA (for himself and Mr. Bennet) submitted an 
amendment intended to be proposed by him

[[Page S1975]]

to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT DOES NOT 
                   PROTECT POLLING LOCATIONS AND ELECTION 
                   ADMINISTRATION SITES FROM UNLAWFUL INTERFERENCE 
                   BY FEDERAL LAW ENFORCEMENT.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or a conference report on or amendment between 
     the Houses in relation to such a bill or joint resolution, if 
     such bill, joint resolution, or conference report does not 
     further ensure protection of polling locations and election 
     administration sites from unlawful interference by Federal 
     law enforcement as proscribed by sections 592, 593 and 594 of 
     title 18, United States Code, and section 11 of the Voting 
     Rights Act of 1965.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______

him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO RELIEF 
                   FOR FARMERS AFFECTED BY INCREASED INPUT COSTS 
                   DUE TO THE WAR WITH IRAN.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     providing relief for farmers affected by increased costs of 
     fertilizer, fuel, and other inputs as a result of the war 
     with Iran by the amounts provided in such legislation for 
     those purposes, provided that such legislation would not 
     increase the deficit over the period of the total of fiscal 
     years 2026 through 2035.

  SA 5001. Mr. MARKEY proposed an amendment to the concurrent 
resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for 
the United States Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the 
appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT WOULD NOT 
                   DECREASE HOME ELECTRICITY BILLS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     not decrease home electricity bills.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5002. Mr. MARKEY submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT WOULD FAIL 
                   TO ADDRESS RISING GASOLINE COSTS AND INCREASE 
                   FLEETWIDE FUEL ECONOMY.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     fail to address rising gasoline costs and increase fleetwide 
     fuel economy.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______

2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   PREVENTING ACTIONS TO BLOCK THE SCHEDULED 
                   RETIREMENT OF ELECTRIC GENERATORS IN CASES IN 
                   WHICH CONTINUED OPERATION WOULD RESULT IN 
                   INCREASED ELECTRICITY COSTS FOR RATEPAYERS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     preventing any actions that would block the scheduled 
     retirement of electric generators in cases in which continued 
     operation would result in increased electricity costs for 
     ratepayers, except in cases in which the relevant grid 
     operator has made, through established procedures at the 
     Federal Energy Regulatory Commission, a formal finding that 
     continued operation is necessary to address a specific and 
     acute reliability risk that cannot be addressed through 
     lower-cost alternatives, by the amounts provided in such 
     legislation for those purposes, provided that such 
     legislation would not increase the deficit over the period of 
     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

         At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT REDUCES 
                   HEALTH CARE PROVIDERS AT THE DEPARTMENT OF 
                   VETERANS AFFAIRS.

         (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the 
     Senate to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, 
     amendment, amendment between the Houses, or conference report 
     that reduces the number of health care providers at the 
     Department of Veterans Affairs.
         (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 5030. Mr. KIM submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4109. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   THAT FUNDS ICE OR CBP BEFORE THE CONCLUSION OF 
                   AN INDEPENDENT AND UNBIASED INVESTIGATION INTO 
                   THE DEATHS OF RENEE GOOD AND ALEX PRETTI.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that would provide funding through budget 
     reconciliation for U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement 
     or U.S. Customs and Border Protection before the conclusion 
     of an independent and unbiased investigation into the deaths 
     of United States citizens Renee Good and Alex Pretti during 
     President Trump's ``Operation Metro Surge'' in Minnesota.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5031. Mr. KIM submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST CONSIDERATION OF 
                   RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION WHEN INFLATION IS 
                   ABOVE 2.0 PERCENT.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a reconciliation bill or a reconciliation 
     resolution pursuant to section 310 of the Congressional 
     Budget and Impoundment Control Act of 1974 (2 U.S.C. 644), or 
     an amendment to, conference report on, or amendment between 
     the Houses in relation to such a bill or resolution, that 
     would provide for the consideration of budget reconciliation 
     legislation when inflation is above 2.0 percent, pursuant to 
     the Consumer Price Index as established by the Bureau of 
     Labor Statistics.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under

  SA 5034. Mr. KIM (for himself and Mr. Booker) submitted an amendment 
intended to be proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. 
Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for the United States 
Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate 
budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered 
to lie on the table; as follows:

       At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4109. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST ANY LEGISLATION THAT 
                   PROVIDES FUNDING FOR U.S. IMMIGRATION AND 
                   CUSTOMS ENFORCEMENT OR U.S. CUSTOMS AND BORDER 
                   PROTECTION UNLESS THE RELEVANT AGENCY HAS 
                   PUBLICLY SHARED ANY AND ALL ENVIRONMENTAL 
                   IMPACT ANALYSES AND STATEMENTS REGARDING 
                   FACILITIES SUCH AGENCY IS OPERATING OR INTENDS 
                   TO PURCHASE, LEASE, CONSTRUCT, OR RETROFIT FOR 
                   USE AS IMMIGRATION DETENTION FACILITIES.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     provide any funding for U.S. Immigration and Customs 
     Enforcement or U.S. Customs and Border Protection unless the 
     relevant agency has made publicly available any and all 
     environmental impact analyses and statements regarding 
     facilities such agency is operating or intends to purchase, 
     lease, construct, or retrofit for immigration detention 
     purposes.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

intended to be proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. 
Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for the United States 
Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate 
budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered 
to lie on the table; as follows:

       At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4109. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT WOULD 
                   PROVIDE ANY FUNDING FOR U.S. IMMIGRATION AND 
                   CUSTOMS ENFORCEMENT OR U.S. CUSTOMS AND BORDER 
                   PROTECTION TO PURCHASE, LEASE, OPERATE, 
                   MAINTAIN, STAFF, OR UTILIZE A WAREHOUSE FOR USE 
                   AS AN IMMIGRATION DETENTION FACILITY.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     provide funding to U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement 
     or U.S. Customs and Border Protection for the purpose of 
     purchasing, leasing, operating, maintaining, staffing, or 
     utilizing a warehouse for use as an immigration detention 
     facility.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

                   NOT ONE MORE RURAL HOSPITAL IS FORCED TO CLOSE.

         The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between

     ensuring that not one more rural hospital is forced to close, 
     which may include redirecting and rescinding funds 
     appropriated under sections 90003 and 100052 of Public Law 
     119-21 (commonly known as the ``One Big Beautiful Bill 
     Act''), including rescinding funds appropriated for the 
     Department of Homeland Security, Immigration and Customs 
     Enforcement, or Customs and Border Patrol and reversing 
     funding cuts made under sections 71101 through 71121 of such 
     Act, by the amounts provided in such legislation for those 
     purposes, provided that such legislation would not increase 
     the deficit over the period of the total of fiscal years 2026 
     through 2035.

                        TITLE II--RECONCILIATION

     SEC. 2001. RECONCILIATION IN THE HOUSE OF REPRESENTATIVES.

         In the House of Representatives, not later than May 15, 
     2026, the Committee on Agriculture shall report to the House 
     of Representatives changes in laws within its jurisdiction 
     that increase the deficit by not more than $140,000,000,000 
     for the period of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

         In the Senate, not later than May 15, 2026, the Committee 
     on Agriculture, Nutrition, and Forestry shall report to the 
     Senate changes in laws within its jurisdiction that increase 
     the deficit by not more than $140,000,000,000 for the period 
     of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. RESERVE FUND RELATING TO REDUCING HOMELESSNESS.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     reducing homelessness for veterans, families, and 
     individuals, which may include investments in the Homeless 
     Assistance Grants program, the Housing Choice Voucher program 
     under section 8 of the United States Housing Act of 1937 (42 
     U.S.C. 1437f), the Department of Housing and Urban 
     Development Veterans Affairs Supportive Housing program, or 
     other Federal programs, by the amounts provided in such 
     legislation for those purposes.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5044. Ms. MURKOWSKI submitted an amendment intended to be proposed 
by her to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   FUNDING ICE OR CBP BEYOND FISCAL YEAR 2026.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, that appropriates funds to U.S. Immigration 
     and Customs Enforcement or U.S. Customs and Border Protection 
     that remain available for obligation after September 30, 
     2026.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______

  SA 5048. Mr. LUJAN submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S.

[[Page S1984]]

Con. Res. 33, setting forth the congressional budget for the United 
States Government for fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the 
appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which 
was ordered to lie on the table; as follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT DOES NOT 
                   PROVIDE AT LEAST $8,500,000,000 IN ANNUAL 
                   FUNDING FOR EMPLOYEES OF THE TRANSPORTATION 
                   SECURITY ADMINISTRATION.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that does 
     not provide at least $8,500,000,000 in annual funding for 
     employees of the Transportation Security Administration.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST INCREASED BARRIERS TO 
                   AMERICANS EXERCISING THEIR RIGHT TO VOTE.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     increase barriers to Americans exercising their right to 
     vote.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 5085. Ms. ROSEN submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
her to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4109. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST ANY LEGISLATION THAT 
                   EXPLICITLY PERMITS IMMIGRATION ENFORCEMENT 
                   ACTIONS BY DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND SECURITY 
                   OFFICERS AND AGENTS WITHIN 1,000 FEET OF ANY 
                   SENSITIVE LOCATION.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that 
     explicitly permits immigration enforcement actions by 
     Department of Homeland Security officers and agents within 
     1,000 feet of any sensitive location, including schools, 
     medical facilities, places of

[[Page S1989]]

     worship, child care facilities, and courthouses.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or

     scams by requiring virtual currency kiosk operators to 
     register virtual currency kiosks with the Financial Crimes 
     Enforcement Network of the Department of Treasury, provide 
     disclosures and fraud warning on all virtual currency kiosks, 
     provide consumer acknowledgement of disclosures on all 
     virtual currency kiosks, appoint a compliance officer, 
     provide print receipts at all virtual currency kiosks, use

[[Page S1992]]

     blockchain analytics, refund the full transaction for new 
     customers and fees for existing customers for any 
     fraudulently induced transaction, impose daily transaction 
     limits of $1,000 for new customers and $2,000 for existing 
     customers, provide live customer service, and communicate 
     with law enforcement about fraudulent transactions, by the 
     amounts provided in such legislation for those purposes, 
     provided that such legislation would not increase the deficit 
     over the period of the total of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.

  SA 5108. Mr. DURBIN submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST ALLOWING THE SOCIAL 
                   SECURITY TRUST FUNDS TO INVEST IN DIGITAL 
                   ASSETS OR CRYPTO-RELATED INVESTMENTS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that does 
     not prohibit the Social Security Trust Funds from investing 
     in digital assets or any crypto-related investment.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

       Beginning on page 45, strike line 3 and all that follows 
     through page 46, line 20, and insert the following:

                        TITLE II--RECONCILIATION

     SEC. 2001. RECONCILIATION IN THE HOUSE OF REPRESENTATIVES.

       In the House of Representatives, not later than May 15, 
     2026, the Committee on Financial Services shall report to the 
     House of Representatives changes in laws within its 
     jurisdiction that increase the deficit by not more than 
     $140,000,000,000 for the period of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.

     SEC. 2002. RECONCILIATION IN THE SENATE.

       In the Senate, not later than May 15, 2026, the Committee 
     on Banking, Housing, and Urban Affairs shall report to the 
     Senate changes in laws within its jurisdiction that increase 
     the deficit by not more than $140,000,000,000 for the period 
     of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

                        TITLE III--RESERVE FUNDS

     SEC. 3000. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   INCREASING HOUSING SUPPLY.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     increasing homeownership, which may include legislation that 
     supports the construction and preservation of single-family, 
     multi-family, and rental housing to increase homeownership, 
     reduce homelessness, and lower housing costs, by the amounts 
     provided in such legislation for those purposes, provided 
     that such legislation would not increase the deficit over the 
     period of the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

       Beginning on page 45, strike line 3 and all that follows 
     through page 46, line 20, and insert the following:

                        TITLE II--RECONCILIATION

     SEC. 2001. RECONCILIATION IN THE HOUSE OF REPRESENTATIVES.

       In the House of Representatives, not later than May 15, 
     2026, the Committee on Financial Services shall report to the 
     House of Representatives changes in laws within its 
     jurisdiction that increase the deficit by not more than 
     $140,000,000,000 for the period of fiscal years 2026 through 
     2035.

     SEC. 2002. RECONCILIATION IN THE SENATE.

       In the Senate, not later than May 15, 2026, the Committee 
     on Banking, Housing, and Urban Affairs shall report to the 
     Senate changes in laws within its jurisdiction that increase 
     the deficit by not more than $140,000,000,000 for the period 
     of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

                        TITLE III--RESERVE FUNDS

     SEC. 3000. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   INCREASING HOMEOWNERSHIP.

     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees,

     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     increasing homeownership, which may include legislation that 
     lowers housing costs by providing downpayment assistance to 
     first-time homebuyers, by the amounts provided in such 
     legislation for those purposes, provided that such 
     legislation would not increase the deficit over the period of 
     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

  SA 5119. Mr. WARNOCK submitted an amendment intended to be proposed 
by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST THE USE OF FUNDS FOR THE 
                   ACQUISITION, CONSTRUCTION, RENOVATION, OR 
                   EXPANSION OF ANY DETENTION FACILITY OF U.S. 
                   IMMIGRATION AND CUSTOMS ENFORCEMENT IN SOCIAL 
                   CIRCLE OR OAKWOOD, GEORGIA.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     permit the use of funds for the acquisition, construction, 
     renovation, or expansion of any detention facility of U.S. 
     Immigration and Customs Enforcement in Social Circle or 
     Oakwood, Georgia.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5120. Mr. WARNOCK submitted an amendment intended to be proposed 
by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST THE USE OF FUNDS TO SUPPORT 
                   IMMIGRATION ENFORCEMENT AT SENSITIVE LOCATIONS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that would 
     permit the use of funds to support immigration enforcement at 
     sensitive locations, such as schools, churches, and polling 
     locations.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

  SA 5135. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

               TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS IN BOTH HOUSES

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT REGARDING LIMITS ON PAYMENT OF 
                   SETTLEMENTS TO INDIVIDUALS WHO ATTEMPTED TO 
                   OVERTURN OR OTHERWISE INTERFERE WITH THE 
                   CERTIFICATION OF THE RESULTS OF THE 2020 
                   ELECTION.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that no 
     Federal funds be obligated or expended for any legal 
     settlement or reimbursement of legal fees to an individual 
     who attempted to overturn or otherwise interfere with the 
     certification of the results of the 2020 election, regardless 
     of whether the individual was granted a pardon by the 
     President.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5136. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

                       TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT ON MEDICAID WORK REQUIREMENTS.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that if the 
     Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services does not issue the 
     interim final rule on Medicaid work requirements required by 
     Public Law 119-21 by June 1, 2026, implementation of such 
     work requirement shall be postponed for 1 year.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5137. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth

[[Page S1996]]

the congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal 
year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST RECONCILIATION LEGISLATION 
                   WHILE THE PRESIDENT RAISES COSTS FOR FAMILIES 
                   IN THE UNITED STATES BY ARBITRARILY DELAYING 
                   REVIEWS AND APPROVALS OF LOW-COST WIND, SOLAR, 
                   AND BATTERY STORAGE PROJECTS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a bill or joint resolution reported pursuant to 
     section 2002, or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill or 
     joint resolution, while the President raises costs for 
     families in the United States by arbitrarily delaying reviews 
     and approvals of low-cost wind, solar, and battery storage 
     projects.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5138. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

               TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS IN BOTH HOUSES

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT REGARDING THE INVESTIGATION OF 
                   THREATS AGAINST FEDERAL JUDGES AND THEIR 
                   FAMILIES.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that no 
     Federal funds be obligated or expended by the Department of 
     Justice until the Attorney General of the United States 
     provides to Congress a written certification confirming that 
     the Department of Justice has ongoing investigations of 
     threats against Federal judges and the family members of 
     Federal judges for evidence of coordination that may 
     constitute a criminal conspiracy, enterprise, or 
     solicitation.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5139. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

               TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS IN BOTH HOUSES

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT REGARDING THE INVESTIGATION INTO 
                   TOM HOMAN.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that no 
     Federal funds be obligated or expended for operations of the 
     Department of Justice until the Department of Justice and the 
     Federal Bureau of Investigation disclose complete records 
     related to the investigation into Tom Homan for accepting 
     $50,000 from undercover agents of the Federal Bureau of 
     Investigation and fully explain the basis for the termination 
     of the investigation.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5140. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

                       TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT ON THE DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND 
                   SECURITY'S ADVERTISING CAMPAIGN DURING THE 
                   SHUTDOWN.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that it was 
     improper for the Department of Homeland Security, in the 
     midst of a lapse in appropriations, to expend $200,000,000 on 
     an advertising campaign that was contracted to a consulting 
     firm with close personal and business ties to the then-
     Secretary of Homeland Security Kristi Noem.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5141. Mr. WHITEHOUSE submitted an amendment intended to be 
proposed by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting 
forth the congressional budget for the United States Government for 
fiscal year 2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for 
fiscal years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end, add the following:

                       TITLE V--POLICY STATEMENTS

     SEC. 5001. POLICY STATEMENT ON THE LUXURY JET PURCHASED BY 
                   THE DEPARTMENT OF HOMELAND SECURITY.

       It is the policy of this concurrent resolution that it was 
     improper--
       (1) for the Department of Homeland Security to expend 
     $70,000,000 on a luxury jet, which included a bedroom with a 
     queen-sized bed, to be used by then-Secretary of Homeland 
     Security Kristi Noem for executive travel when the Department 
     already had sufficient travel accommodations for the 
     Secretary; and
       (2) for the White House to then commandeer that jet for use 
     by select Cabinet Secretaries and the First Lady.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 5142. Mr. PADILLA submitted an amendment intended to be proposed 
by him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the end of title III, add the following:

     SEC. 3___. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   PROHIBITING RACIAL OR ETHNIC PROFILING IN 
                   IMMIGRATION ENFORCEMENT.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees, 
     aggregates, and other appropriate levels in this resolution, 
     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or 
     more bills, joint resolutions, amendments, amendments between 
     the Houses, motions, or conference reports relating to 
     prohibiting immigration enforcement actions in which race, 
     ethnicity, nationality, English proficiency, or any other 
     perceived or actual protected characteristic are used as the 
     sole basis to form a reasonable suspicion to stop, arrest, or 
     detain any individual, by the amounts provided in such 
     legislation for those purposes, provided that such 
     legislation would not increase the deficit over the period of 
     the total of fiscal years 2026 through 2035.

  SA 5169. Mr. KAINE submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST LEGISLATION THAT 
                   POLITICIZES THE CIVIL SERVICE.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider any bill, joint resolution, motion, amendment, 
     amendment between the Houses, or conference report that funds 
     the implementation of Executive Order 14171 (90 Fed. Reg. 
     8625; relating to restoring accountability to policy-
     influencing positions within the Federal workforce).
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).

     SEC. 3003. DEFICIT-NEUTRAL RESERVE FUND RELATING TO 
                   PREVENTING RACIAL PROFILING BY U.S. IMMIGRATION 
                   AND CUSTOMS ENFORCEMENT AND U.S. BORDER PATROL.

       The Chairman of the Committee on the Budget of the Senate 
     may revise the allocations of a committee or committees,

     and make adjustments to the pay-as-you-go ledger, for one or

  SA 5171. Mr. KAINE submitted an amendment intended to be proposed by 
him to the concurrent resolution S. Con. Res. 33, setting forth the 
congressional budget for the United States Government for fiscal year 
2026 and setting forth the appropriate budgetary levels for fiscal 
years 2027 through 2035; which was ordered to lie on the table; as 
follows:

       At the appropriate place in title IV, add the following:

     SEC. 4___. POINT OF ORDER AGAINST INCREASING THE DEFICIT 
                   THROUGH RECONCILIATION MULTIPLE TIMES DURING A 
                   SINGLE CONGRESS.

       (a) Point of Order.--It shall not be in order in the Senate 
     to consider a reconciliation bill pursuant to section 310 of 
     the Congressional Budget and Impoundment Control Act of 1974 
     (2 U.S.C. 644), or an amendment to, conference report on, or 
     amendment between the Houses in relation to such a bill, that 
     would cause or increase a deficit or reduce a surplus in any 
     year if--
       (1) the Congressional Budget Office estimated that a prior 
     reconciliation bill considered during the same Congress 
     pursuant to section 310 of the Congressional Budget and 
     Impoundment Control Act of 1974 (2 U.S.C. 644) (as amended, 
     if amended) would cause or increase a deficit or reduce a 
     surplus in any year; and
       (2) that reconciliation bill was enacted into law.
       (b) Waiver and Appeal.--Subsection (a) may be waived or 
     suspended in the Senate only by an affirmative vote of three-
     fifths of the Members, duly chosen and sworn. An affirmative 
     vote of three-fifths of the Members of the Senate, duly 
     chosen and sworn, shall be required to sustain an appeal of 
     the ruling of the Chair on a point of order raised under 
     subsection (a).
       (c) Determination of Budget Levels.--For purposes of this 
     section, the levels of net deficit increases and net surplus 
     reduction shall be determined on the basis of estimates 
     prepared by the Congressional Budget Office that are provided 
     by the Committee on the Budget of the Senate.
                                 ______
```
